as we just demonstrated. Hence (
D.5
) controls all deviations larger than
\omega^{\prime}\eta_{1}
while (
D.4
) controls all deviations larger than
\omega^{\prime}\eta_{\tilde{J}}
. Therefore, for all
y\geq\eta_{\tilde{J}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|>\omega^{\prime}y|S\neq\varnothing)

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\tfrac{N\kappa{\omega^{\prime}}^{2}\gamma y^{2}}{32C_{8}^{2}\sigma^{2}}\right)\vee

\displaystyle\quad\quad\left(\exp\left(-\tfrac{NC_{6}(\kappa)y^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)+4\cdot\mathbbm{1}(J>1)\exp\left(-\tfrac{Ny^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq 7\exp\left(-\tfrac{NC_{9}(\kappa)y^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right),

where
C_{9}(\kappa)
is a constant that depends on
\kappa,C_{8},\omega^{\prime},\gamma
, i.e., only
\kappa
and
C
.

Part IV: Bounding
\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}|S\neq\varnothing\right]
.

Integrate as before:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}|S\neq\varnothing\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}>x|S\neq\varnothing)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=2{\omega^{\prime 2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}u\cdot\mathbb{P}(\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|>\omega^{\prime}u|S\neq\varnothing)\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\leq 2{\omega^{\prime 2}}\int_{0}^{\eta_{\tilde{J}}}u\cdot\mathrm{d}u+2{\omega^{\prime 2}}\int_{\eta_{\tilde{J}}}^{\infty}u\cdot\mathbb{P}(\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|>\omega^{\prime}u)\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle\leq{\omega^{\prime}}^{2}\eta_{\tilde{J}}^{2}+14{\omega^{\prime}}^{2}\int_{\eta_{\tilde{J}}}^{\infty}u\cdot\exp(-\tfrac{NC_{9}(\kappa)u^{2}}{\sigma^{2}})\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle={\omega^{\prime}}^{2}\eta_{\tilde{J}}^{2}+14{\omega^{\prime}}^{2}\cdot\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{2NC_{9}(\kappa)}\exp\left(-\tfrac{NC_{9}(\kappa)\eta_{\tilde{J}}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{(19+16C)^{2}}{4C_{3}(\kappa)}\eta_{\tilde{J}}^{2}+\tfrac{7(19+16C)^{2}}{2C_{3}(\kappa)}\cdot\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{2NC_{9}(\kappa)}\exp\left(-\tfrac{NC_{9}(\kappa)\eta_{\tilde{J}}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right).

(D.6)

Part V: Bounding
\mathbb{P}(2^{k-1}R<\hat{R}\leq 2^{k}R])
and
\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{p}-\mu\|^{2}|2^{k-1}R<\hat{R}\leq 2^{k}R]
.

On the other hand, let us consider the scenario where
S(R)=\varnothing
. In this case, recall we set the estimator to the smallest point lexicographically in
\hat{p}\in S(\hat{R})
(see main text). Now we perform a so-called peeling argument on
\hat{R}
. For any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, define
p_{k}:=\mathbb{P}(2^{k-1}R<\hat{R}\leq 2^{k}R)
. Let
p_{0}
be the probability
\mathbb{P}(S\neq\varnothing)=\mathbb{P}(\hat{R}\leq R)
. If
p_{0}=1
, then we immediately obtain
\mathbb{E}_{X}[\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}|S\neq\varnothing]=\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}
and we can apply our previous bound from (
D.6
). Otherwise, we assume
p_{0}<1
so that for some
k\in\mathbb{N}
,
p_{k}>0
. Pick one such
k
. Then observe

\displaystyle p_{k}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\hat{R}\leq 2^{k}R)-\mathbb{P}(\hat{R}\leq 2^{k-1}R)\leq 1-\mathbb{P}(\mu\in S(2^{k-1}R))

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\tfrac{N(1/2-\epsilon)\gamma(2^{k-1}R)^{2}}{32\sigma^{2}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\tfrac{N\kappa\gamma(2^{k-1}R)^{2}}{32\sigma^{2}}\right).

The first inequality holds since if
\mu\in S(2^{k-1}R)
, then
S(2^{k-1}R)\neq\varnothing
so
\hat{R}\leq 2^{k-1}R
by definition as a minimum; the second inequality uses Lemma
5.3
and the third
1/2-\epsilon>\kappa
. Then our estimator
\hat{p}
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\hat{p}-\mu\|>2t|2^{k-1}R<\hat{R}\leq 2^{k}R)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}(\|\hat{p}-\mu\|>2t,2^{k-1}R<\hat{R}\leq 2^{k}R)}{p_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{P}(\|\hat{p}-\mu\|>2t,\hat{p}\in S(2^{k}R))}{p_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{P}(\mu\not\in S(t))}{p_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp\left(-\tfrac{N\kappa\gamma t^{2}}{32\sigma^{2}}\right)}{p_{k}}

(D.7)

for all
t\geq 2^{k}R
. The second line follows since
\hat{R}\leq 2^{k}R
implies
\hat{p}\in S(\hat{R})\subseteq S(2^{k}R)
by Lemma
5.4
, and the third since
S(2^{k}R)\subseteq S(t)
with
\mathrm{diam}(S(t))\leq 2t
so both
\hat{p},\mu
cannot belong to
S(t)
if
\|\hat{p}-\mu\|>2t
. The fourth again applied Lemma
5.3
and that
1/2-\epsilon>\kappa
.

Now observe that for values of
t\geq C_{10}(\kappa)(\sigma/\sqrt{N})\sqrt{\log 1/p_{k}}
where
C_{10}(\kappa)=\sqrt{32/(\kappa\gamma)}
, the above bound is meaningful (i.e., smaller than
1
). We integrate our tail bound in (
D.7
) and obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{p}-\mu\|^{2}|2^{k-1}R<\hat{R}\leq 2^{k}R]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}(\|\hat{p}-\mu\|^{2}>u|2^{k-1}R<\hat{R}\leq 2^{k}R)\mathrm{d}u

\displaystyle=4\int_{0}^{\infty}2t\cdot\mathbb{P}(\|\hat{p}-\mu\|>2t|2^{k-1}R<\hat{R}\leq 2^{k}R)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 8\int_{0}^{C_{10}(\kappa)(\sigma/\sqrt{N})\sqrt{\log 1/p_{k}}\vee(2^{k}R)}t\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\quad\quad+8\int_{C_{10}(\kappa)(\sigma/\sqrt{N})\sqrt{\log 1/p_{k}}\vee(2^{k}R)}^{\infty}t\cdot\tfrac{\exp\left(-\tfrac{N\kappa\gamma t^{2}}{32\sigma^{2}}\right)}{p_{k}}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim(2^{k}R)^{2}\vee\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\log\tfrac{1}{p_{k}}+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}.

(D.8)

To elaborate, we first split up the integral into two portions, where in the first region, we simply bound the probability by
1
, and in the second, we apply our tail bound. The first integral is of a linear function and integrates easily to get the
(2^{k}R)^{2}\vee\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\log\tfrac{1}{p_{k}}
expression. The second is more elaborate. Ignoring the constants, we perform a substitution
u=\sqrt{N}t/\sigma
, and then rescale the integrand and bounds of integration. We obtain an integral of the form
\int_{a}^{\infty}u\exp(-u^{2})du\asymp\exp(-a^{2})
where
a=\sqrt{\log 1/p_{k}}\vee(2^{k}R\sqrt{N}/\sigma)
. This gives us a bound of

\displaystyle\frac{\sigma^{2}}{Np_{k}}\exp\left(-\left[\log(1/p_{k})\vee(2^{k}R\sqrt{N}/\sigma)^{2}\right]\right).

(D.9)

But notice

\log(1/p_{k})\vee(2^{k}R\sqrt{N}/\sigma)^{2}\geq\log(1/p_{k}),

hence

-\left[\log(1/p_{k})\vee(2^{k}R\sqrt{N}/\sigma)^{2}\right]\leq-\log(1/p_{k}).

Exponentiating both sides, we conclude

\exp\left(-\left[\log(1/p_{k})\vee(2^{k}R\sqrt{N}/\sigma)^{2}\right]\right)\leq\exp(-\log(1/p_{k}))=p_{k}.

Thus, the bound in (
D.9
) reduces to
\sigma^{2}/N
as claimed.

Part VI: Bounding
\mathbb{E}\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}
.

By the law of total expectation, the risk of our estimator is exactly

\mathbb{E}[\|\nu^{**}-\mu\|^{2}|S\neq\varnothing]\cdot p_{0}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{N}\\
p_{k}>0\end{subarray}}p_{k}\cdot\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{p}-\mu\|^{2}|2^{k-1}R<\hat{R}\leq 2^{k}R].

(D.10)

Note that we have already bounded
\mathbb{E}[\|\nu^{**}-\mu\|^{2}|S\neq\varnothing]
in (
D.6
). Using (
D.8
), the second term in (
D.10
) is bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{N}\\
p_{k}>0\end{subarray}}p_{k}\left[(2^{k}R)^{2}\vee\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\log\tfrac{1}{p_{k}}+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{N}\\
p_{k}>0\end{subarray}}\exp\left(-\tfrac{N(1/2-\epsilon)\gamma(2^{k-1}R)^{2}}{32\sigma^{2}}\right)\cdot(2^{k}R)^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{N}\\
p_{k}>0\end{subarray}}p_{k}\cdot\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\log\tfrac{1}{p_{k}}+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{N}\\
p_{k}>0\end{subarray}}\exp\left(-\tfrac{N(1/2-\epsilon)\gamma(2^{k-1}R)^{2}}{32\sigma^{2}}\right)(2^{k}R)^{2}\tfrac{N}{\sigma^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\mathbb{N}\\
p_{k}>0\end{subarray}}\left[\exp\left(-\tfrac{N(1/2-\epsilon)\gamma(2^{k-1}R)^{2}}{32\sigma^{2}}\right)\tfrac{N(2^{k-1}R)^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right]+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}.

The first inequality above follows by
a\vee b\leq a+b
and
\sum p_{k}\leq 1
. The second inequality comes from bounding the second term as follows: we observe that
x\mapsto x\log(1/x)
is increasing on
(0,1/e)
and also that
p_{k}\leq\exp\left(-\tfrac{N(1/2-\epsilon)\gamma(2^{k-1}R)^{2}}{32\sigma^{2}}\right)\leq 1/e
(using the third requirement on
R
in (
5.1
)). Thus, we can replace the
p_{k}
in
p_{k}\log\tfrac{1}{p_{k}}
with
\exp\left(-\tfrac{N(1/2-\epsilon)\gamma(2^{k-1}R)^{2}}{32\sigma^{2}}\right)
, and discard the
1/2-\epsilon<1/2
(and other constants) in the
\log(1/p_{k})
term. The last inequality follows by first noting we have can bound each of the first two terms with summations of the form
\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\exp(-4^{k}c)(2^{k}c)^{2}
where
c=\tfrac{N(1/2-\epsilon)\gamma R^{2}}{4\cdot 32\sigma^{2}}
. Using the third requirement of
R
, we can show
c>\tfrac{16N\log 2}{4}\geq 2
. Then using
\exp(-x)\leq x^{-3}
, which holds in particular for all
x\geq 5
(and thus
4^{k}c
), we have

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\exp(-4^{k}c)(2^{k}c)^{2}\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}(4^{k}c)^{-3}\cdot(4^{k}c^{2})=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\tfrac{c^{-1}}{16^{k}}\lesssim 1.

Combining this result with (
D.10
) and our previous bound from (
D.6
), we conclude

\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}\lesssim\eta_{\tilde{J}}^{2}+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\exp\left(-\tfrac{NC_{9}(\kappa)\eta_{\tilde{J}}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
5.9
.

We claim that (
5.4
) must hold for
J=1
, for suppose not. Recall
d_{m}\geq R
and we required in (
5.1
) that
R\geq\tfrac{\sigma\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{1-\gamma}}
. Thus
d_{m}^{2}\geq\tfrac{\sigma^{2}n}{1-\gamma}
so by definition of
\eta_{1}
we have

\tfrac{N\eta_{1}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}=\tfrac{Nd_{m}^{2}C_{3}(\kappa)}{(C+1)^{2}\sigma^{2}}\geq\tfrac{C_{3}(\kappa)Nn}{(C+1)^{2}(1-\gamma)}.

Now if (
5.4
) does not hold, either
\tfrac{N\eta_{1}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}<\log 2
or
\tfrac{N\eta_{1}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}<6\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\tfrac{c\eta_{1}}{\sqrt{C_{3}(\kappa)}},2c).
In the first case, we have
\tfrac{C_{3}(\kappa)Nn}{(C+1)^{2}(1-\gamma)}\leq\log 2
, which means

\tfrac{C_{3}(\kappa)}{(C+1)^{2}\log 2}\leq\tfrac{C_{3}(\kappa)Nn}{(C+1)^{2}\log 2}\leq 1-\gamma\quad\Rightarrow\quad\gamma\leq 1-\tfrac{C_{3}(\kappa)}{(C+1)^{2}\log 2}.

But recall (from the text leading up to (
5.1
)) we assumed
\gamma
exceeds
1-\tfrac{C_{3}(\kappa)}{6(C+1)^{2}\log 2}
, so this cannot occur. In the other case, we have
\tfrac{N\eta_{1}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}<6\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\tfrac{c\eta_{1}}{\sqrt{C_{3}(\kappa)}},2c).
Well from
Wainwright [2019, Example 5.8]

\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\tfrac{c\eta_{1}}{\sqrt{C_{3}(\kappa)}},2c)\leq n\cdot\log\left(1+\tfrac{2\sqrt{C_{3}(\kappa)}}{\eta_{1}}\right)=n\cdot\log\left(1+\tfrac{2(C+1)}{d_{m}}\right).

Note
d_{m}>R
and we assumed
R\geq 2(C+1)
, so that
\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\tfrac{c\eta_{1}}{\sqrt{C_{3}(\kappa)}},2c)\leq n\log 2.
Thus

\tfrac{C_{3}(\kappa)n}{(C+1)^{2}(1-\gamma)}\leq\tfrac{C_{3}(\kappa)Nn}{(C+1)^{2}(1-\gamma)}\leq\tfrac{N\eta_{1}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}<6\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\tfrac{c\eta_{1}}{\sqrt{C_{3}(\kappa)}},2c)\leq 6n\log 2,

i.e.,
1-\gamma>\tfrac{C_{3}(\kappa)}{6(C+1)^{2}\log 2}
which implies
\gamma<1-\tfrac{C_{3}(\kappa)}{6(C+1)^{2}\log 2}
. But we required
\gamma>1-\tfrac{C_{3}(\kappa)}{6(C+1)^{2}\log 2}
, so this cannot occur. Hence, (
5.4
) holds for
J=1
.

We may now proceed as in the bounded proof by considering two cases. We check whether the
\frac{d_{m}}{2^{J-1}(C+1)}\geq C_{1}(\kappa)\epsilon\sigma
condition fails for
J\leq J^{\ast}
or not. If it does fail, we additionally consider two sub-cases where
\epsilon\gtreqless\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
.

Case 1:
Assume
\frac{d_{m}}{2^{J^{\ast}-1}(C+1)}\geq C_{1}(\kappa)\epsilon\sigma
. Then by Lemma
D.2
with
\tilde{J}=J^{\ast}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2}+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\exp\left(-\tfrac{NC_{9}(\kappa)\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}.

(D.11)

The exponential term is
\leq 1
. Moreover, (
5.4
) implies
\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}<\tfrac{\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2}}{\log 2}
. Thus, we bound (
D.11
) up to constants depending on
C
and
\kappa
with

\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2}+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\lesssim\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2}\lesssim\max(\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2},\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}),

completing the first case.

Case 2
: Suppose for some
J^{\prime}\in\{1,2,\dots,J^{\ast}\}

\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J^{\prime}-1}(C+1)}<C_{1}(\kappa)\epsilon\sigma.

(D.12)

If
J^{\prime}=1
, then
d_{m}\leq(C+1)C_{1}(\kappa)\epsilon\sigma
. But we also know
d_{m}>R\geq\tfrac{\sigma\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{1-\gamma}}
and
\epsilon<1/2
, which implies

\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\leq\tfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\leq(C+1)C_{1}(\kappa)\epsilon\leq\tfrac{(C+1)C_{1}(\kappa)}{2}.

After some rearranging, this is equivalent to
\gamma\leq 1-\tfrac{4}{(C+1)^{2}C_{1}^{2}(\kappa)}
. But we already assumed
\gamma>1-\tfrac{4}{(C+1)^{2}C_{1}^{2}(\kappa)}
, yielding a contradiction. Thus, we assume
J^{\prime}>1
and choose it minimally. Then for all
1\leq J\leq J^{\prime}-1
, we have
\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J-1}(C+1)}\geq C_{1}(\kappa)\epsilon\sigma
. Applying Lemma
D.2
with
\tilde{J}=J^{\prime}-1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta_{J^{\prime}-1}^{2}+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\exp\left(-\tfrac{NC_{9}(\kappa)\eta_{J^{\prime}-1}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}.

(D.13)

Now pick
C_{5}(\kappa)
to be some positive absolute constant less than
\tfrac{\sqrt{\log 2}}{\sqrt{C_{3}(\kappa)}C_{1}(\kappa)}
. Let us compare
\epsilon
to
\tfrac{C_{5}(\kappa)}{\sqrt{N}}
.

Case 2(a)
: Suppose
\epsilon\leq\tfrac{C_{5}(\kappa)}{\sqrt{N}}
. Then
\epsilon\sigma\leq\tfrac{C_{5}(\kappa)\sigma}{\sqrt{N}}
. By definition of
J^{\prime}
in (
D.12
),

\displaystyle\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J^{\prime}-1}}<C_{1}(\kappa)(C+1)\epsilon\sigma\leq C_{5}(\kappa)C_{1}(\kappa)(C+1)\cdot\tfrac{\sigma}{\sqrt{N}}.

Applying the definition of
\eta_{J}
,

\tfrac{N\eta_{J^{\prime}-1}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\leq\left[\sqrt{C_{3}(\kappa)}C_{5}(\kappa)C_{1}(\kappa)\right]^{2}<\log 2.

Thus, (
5.4
) does not hold, and again by maximality of
J^{\ast}
, we have
J^{\prime}>J^{\ast}
. This means
J^{\prime}=J^{\ast}
. But (
5.4
) not holding for
J^{\ast}
implies
J^{\ast}=1
and hence
J^{\prime}=1
, which we already handled.

Case 2(b)
: Assume
\epsilon\geq\tfrac{C_{5}(\kappa)}{\sqrt{N}}
. Then
\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\geq\tfrac{C_{5}(\kappa)^{2}\sigma^{2}}{N}
. Also, the exponential term is
\leq 1
. Also, by definition of
J^{\prime}
in (
D.12
),

\eta_{J^{\prime}-1}=2\cdot\eta_{J^{\prime}}=\tfrac{2\sqrt{C_{3}(\kappa)}}{C+1}\cdot\tfrac{d}{2^{J^{\prime}-1}}<2\sqrt{C_{3}(\kappa)}C_{1}(\kappa)\epsilon\sigma.

So our expression in (
D.13
) is
\lesssim\eta_{J^{\prime}-1}^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\lesssim\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}\leq\max(\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2},\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2})
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.10
.

Note that
\eta^{\ast}>0
, since if
\eta^{\ast}=0
, then for all sufficiently small
\eta
we have
\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\eta,c)=1
, which is impossible since
K
is an unbounded star-shaped set.

Case 1:

\epsilon\geq\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
.
Observe

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\eta^{\ast}/4,c)

\displaystyle\geq\lim_{\gamma\rightarrow 0}\log\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\eta^{\ast}-\gamma,c)\geq\lim_{\gamma\rightarrow 0}\tfrac{N{(\eta^{\ast}-\gamma)}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\geq 2\cdot\tfrac{N(\eta^{\ast}/4)^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}.

Thus, by the remarks in Section
5.1
about Lemma
2.1
, the minimax rate is lower bounded by
{\eta^{\ast}}^{2}
up to constants. Moreover, since
\epsilon\geq\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
, we have
\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2}
as a lower bound. Hence
\max({\eta^{\ast}}^{2},\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2})
is a lower bound.

On the other hand, we have
\max(\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2},\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2})
as an upper bound from Theorem
5.9
. Note by Remark
3.9
, we can swap out the
2c
in (
5.4
) with
c
. Now, observe that if
c
is sufficiently large, then the
\log 2
term in (
5.4
) can be dropped (since the local entropy term can be made large by taking points along line segments of arbitrary long lengths in
K
by the unbounded star-shaped property). Then repeat the argument from Case 1 of the proof of Theorem
3.10
with minor changes. We take
\beta^{\prime}=\min(\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}},\tfrac{c}{2}\sqrt{\tfrac{2}{C_{3}(\kappa)}})\in(0,1/\sqrt{3}]
so that we get a factor of 6 instead of 4 in (
B.20
) (which allows us to get the desired
2\log\left[\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}\left(\frac{c\tilde{\eta}}{2\sqrt{C_{3}(\kappa)}},c\right)\right]^{3}
lower bound). Taking the same
\tilde{\eta}\asymp\eta^{\ast}
such that
{\tilde{\eta}}^{2}\geq{\eta_{J^{\ast}}}^{2}
, disregarding the
\log 2
requirement, and defining the analogous non-decreasing map
\phi
without the
\log 2
term and with a cubic power on the metric entropy. Ultimately, we conclude
\max(\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2},\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2})\lesssim\max(\eta_{\ast}^{2},\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2})
is an upper bound, matching our lower bound.

Case 2:

\epsilon\leq\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
. The upper bound argument in Case 1 still holds, so it remains to prove the lower bound. We also have the lower bound of
{\eta^{\ast}}^{2}
by the same argument in Case 1. It suffices to show that
\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}
is a lower bound. Well, if we take
\eta\asymp\epsilon\sigma
, then
\tfrac{2N\eta^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\lesssim 1
, and we know for sufficiently large
c
that the local entropy can be made arbitrarily large. So again by the remarks in Section
5.1
about the extension of Lemma
2.1
, the required hypothesis holds and
\epsilon^{2}\sigma^{2}
is indeed a lower bound.
∎

We now proceed to the unbounded sub-Gaussian setting. Due to similarities with the unbounded Gaussian setting, we skip most of the details in Lemma
D.3
. Lemma
D.4
has an identical proof to Lemma
D.2
with some minor differences of constants which we explain how to handle.

Lemma D.3
.

Consider the sub-Gaussian noise setting. Let
\eta_{J}
be defined as in Theorem
5.11
and let
C_{1},C_{3},C_{5}
be given from Theorem
4.5
. Set
C_{7}=\frac{15\gamma C^{2}}{256\cdot 32C_{5}}
. Suppose
\tilde{J}
is such that (
5.5
) holds, that
\frac{d_{m}}{2^{\tilde{J}-1}(C+1)}\geq C_{1}\sigma\epsilon\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}
, and additionally that
\tfrac{\sigma}{\sqrt{N}}\leq\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{\tilde{J}-1}(C+1)}
. Then for each
1\leq J\leq\tilde{J}
we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\|\Upsilon_{J}-\mu\|\geq\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J-1}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\tfrac{NC_{7}\eta_{J}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)+4\cdot\mathbbm{1}(J>1)\exp(-\tfrac{N\eta_{J}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}).

Proof.

As with the bounded sub-Gaussian case compared to the bounded Gaussian cases, the details are identical except we substitute a different assumption about
\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J-1}}
and obtain different absolute constants. We will also use the tournament bound in Lemma
4.6
instead.

Repeating the logic, we will again obtain
\mathbb{P}(A_{1}|S\neq\varnothing)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{N(1/2-\epsilon)\gamma R^{2}}{32\sigma^{2}}\right)
, that

\mathbb{P}(A_{2}\cap A_{1}^{c}|S\neq\varnothing)\leq 2\left[\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(d_{m},2c)\right]^{3}\exp(-\tfrac{N\eta_{2}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}),

and for
j\geq 3
,

\mathbb{P}(A_{j}\cap A_{j-1}^{c}\cap A_{1}^{c}|S\neq\varnothing)\leq 2\left[\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{j-2}},2c)\right]^{3}\exp(-\tfrac{N\eta_{j}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}).

Hence carrying through the usual calculations along with Lemma
B.5
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|\Upsilon_{J}-\mu\|>\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J-1}}|S\neq\varnothing)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\tfrac{N(1/2-\epsilon)\cdot\gamma R^{2}}{32\sigma^{2}}\right)+4\cdot\mathbbm{1}(J>1)\exp\left(-\tfrac{N\eta_{J}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right).

As we argued in the Gaussian case with slightly different constants, using the definition of
\eta_{J}
we have
R=\tfrac{2^{J-1}(2C+1)\eta_{J}}{4\sqrt{C_{5}}}\geq\tfrac{C\eta_{J}}{4\sqrt{C_{5}}}
, hence

\exp\left(-\tfrac{N(1/2-\epsilon)\cdot\gamma R^{2}}{32\sigma^{2}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\tfrac{N(1/2-\epsilon)\cdot\gamma C^{2}\eta_{J}^{2}}{512C_{5}\sigma^{2}}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\tfrac{15N\gamma C^{2}\eta_{J}^{2}}{512\cdot 32C_{5}\sigma^{2}}\right).

The last step used that
\epsilon<1/32
in the sub-Gaussian case, so
1/2-\epsilon>15/32
. Then set
C_{7}=\tfrac{15\gamma C^{2}}{256\cdot 32C_{5}}
, and the bound becomes
\exp\left(-\tfrac{NC_{7}\eta_{J}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)
.
∎

Lemma D.4
.

Consider the sub-Gaussian noise setting. Let
\eta_{J}
be defined as in Theorem
5.11
. Suppose
\tilde{J}
is such that (
5.5
) holds, that
\frac{d_{m}}{2^{\tilde{J}-1}(C+1)}\geq C_{1}\sigma\epsilon\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}
, and additionally
\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{\tilde{J}-1}(C+1)}\geq\tfrac{\sigma}{\sqrt{N}}
. Then if
\nu^{\ast\ast}
denotes the output after at least
J^{\ast}
iterations of Algorithm
2
in the
S(R)\neq\varnothing
case or the smallest lexicographic point in
S(\hat{R})
in the
S(R)=\varnothing
case, for some constant
C_{9}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta_{\tilde{J}}^{2}+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\exp\left(-\tfrac{NC_{9}\eta_{\tilde{J}}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}.

The details of the omitted proof of Lemma
D.4
are extremely similar to the Gaussian unbounded case in Lemma
D.2
, just swapping out constants in most places. However, we explain one possible gap. In the Gaussian unbounded risk bound (Lemma
D.2
), Part I and Part II yielded two bounds:
\mathbb{P}(\|\cdot\|>t)\lesssim\exp(-\Omega(Nt^{2}/\sigma^{2}))
for
t\geq C_{8}R
, and
\mathbb{P}(\|\cdot\|>t)\lesssim\exp(-\Omega(Nt^{2}/\sigma^{2}))
for
t\in[\eta_{\tilde{J}}\omega^{\prime},\eta_{1}\omega^{\prime}]
(omitting the inputs, some constants in the exponential, and the conditioning on
S\neq\varnothing
). Because it happened that
\eta_{1}\omega^{\prime}>C_{8}R
, in Part III we could stitch together a bound for all
t\geq\eta_{\tilde{J}}\omega^{\prime}
. In the sub-Gaussian case, it may happen that
\eta_{1}\omega^{\prime}<C_{8}R
(due to different analogous constants defining each of these terms). Let us explain how the argument can be resolved. Well, for
t\in[\eta_{1}\omega^{\prime},C_{8}R]
, observe that
\mathbb{P}(\|\cdot\|>t)\leq\mathbb{P}(\|\cdot\|>\eta_{1}\omega^{\prime})\lesssim\exp(-\Omega(NR^{2}/\sigma^{2}))\asymp\exp(-Nt^{2}/\sigma^{2})
using our bound for
t\in[\eta_{\tilde{J}}\omega^{\prime},\eta_{1}\omega^{\prime}]
and the fact that
t\asymp R
when
t\in[\eta_{1}\omega^{\prime},C_{8}R]
. Thus, this potential for a gap is a non-issue for our bound even in the sub-Gaussian case.

Another minor remark is that we used
p_{k}<1/e
in Part VI of the Gaussian case, but since Lemma
5.3
applies for both the Gaussian and sub-Gaussian setting, we have the same bound of
p_{k}\leq\exp(-\tfrac{N\gamma(1/2-\epsilon)(2^{k-1}R)^{2}}{32\sigma^{2}})
, and our third requirement on
R
in (
5.1
) still yields
p_{k}<1/e
.

Proof of Theorem
5.11
.

By the same reasoning as in the proof of Theorem
5.9
(but instead requiring
\gamma>1-\tfrac{C_{5}}{6(C+1)^{2}\log 2}
), the condition (
5.5
) must hold for
J=1
, so for all
1\leq J\leq J^{\ast}
(including if
J^{\ast}=1
), we have (
5.5
).

Now we consider 3 cases as in the bounded, sub-Gaussian setting.

Case 1:
Suppose
\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J^{\ast}-1}(C+1)}\geq C_{1}\sigma\epsilon\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}
, and also that
\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J^{\ast}-1}(C+1)}\geq\tfrac{\sigma}{\sqrt{N}}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2}+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\exp\left(-\tfrac{NC_{9}\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}.

by Lemma
D.4
. Then since
\tfrac{N\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}>\log 2
, we have
\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\lesssim\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2}
. The exponential is always
\leq 1
. Hence
\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}\lesssim\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2}\leq\max(\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2},\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2}\log(1/\epsilon))
.

Case 2:
Suppose for some minimally chosen index
1\leq J^{\prime}\leq J^{\ast}
we have
\frac{d_{m}}{2^{J^{\prime}-1}(C+1)}<\tfrac{\sigma}{\sqrt{N}}
.

Case 2(a):
Suppose
J^{\prime}=1
. Then
d_{m}\leq\tfrac{(C+1)\sigma}{\sqrt{N}}
. But we also know
d_{m}>R\geq\tfrac{\sigma\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{1-\gamma}}
, so
\tfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\leq\tfrac{C+1}{\sqrt{N}}
. This means
\sqrt{1-\gamma}\geq\tfrac{\sqrt{Nn}}{C+1}\geq\tfrac{1}{C+1}
, which rearranges to
\gamma\leq 1-\tfrac{1}{(C+1)^{2}}
. But recall we required
\gamma>1-\tfrac{1}{(C+1)^{2}}
, a contradiction. Thus
J^{\prime}\geq 2
.

Case 2(b):
Suppose
J^{\prime}\geq 2
. Then by minimality, each
1\leq J\leq J^{\prime}-1
satisfies
\frac{d_{m}}{2^{J-1}(C+1)}\geq\tfrac{\sigma}{\sqrt{N}}
.

Case 2(b)(i):
Assume there exists some minimally chosen index
1\leq J^{\prime\prime}\leq J^{\prime}-1
such that
\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J^{\prime\prime}-1}(C+1)}<C_{1}\sigma\epsilon\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}
. If
J^{\prime\prime}=1
, then
d_{m}\leq C_{1}(C+1)\sigma\epsilon\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}
. But as we showed in the proof of Theorem
5.9
, we have
d_{m}>R\geq\tfrac{\sigma\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{1-\gamma}}
so

\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\leq\tfrac{\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{1-\gamma}}\leq C_{1}(C+1)\epsilon\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}\leq C_{1}(C+1)

since
\epsilon\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}\leq 1.
This re-arranges to
\gamma\leq 1-\tfrac{1}{C_{1}^{2}(C+1)^{2}}
, in violation of our requirements on
\gamma
.

So suppose
J^{\prime\prime}>1
. Then repeat the argument of Case 2(b)(i) in the proof of Theorem
4.7
in the
J^{\prime\prime}>1
cases, where we replace
d
with
d_{m}
and apply Lemma
D.4
instead to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta_{J^{\prime\prime}-1}^{2}+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\exp\left(-\tfrac{NC_{9}\eta_{J^{\prime\prime}-1}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}.

In one sub-case (
\epsilon\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}\leq C_{6}/\sqrt{N}
where
C_{6}
is defined in the bounded proof), we would conclude (
5.5
) does not hold for
J^{\prime\prime}
, which means
J^{\prime\prime}>J^{\ast}
, a contradiction since
J^{\prime\prime}<J^{\ast}
. In the other sub case, (
\epsilon\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}\geq C_{6}/\sqrt{N}
), note that
\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\lesssim\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2}\log(1/\epsilon)
and that the exponential term is
\leq 1
. Moreover,
\eta_{J^{\prime\prime}-1}=2\eta_{J^{\prime\prime}}\asymp\frac{d_{m}}{2^{J^{\prime\prime}-1}}\lesssim\sigma\epsilon\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}
. So the entire bound on
\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}
is
\lesssim\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2}\log(1/\epsilon)
and thus
\lesssim\max(\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2},\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2}\log(1/\epsilon))
.

Case 2(b)(ii):
In this subcase, we have
\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J^{\prime}-1}(C+1)}<\tfrac{\sigma}{\sqrt{N}}
but for
1\leq J\leq J^{\prime}-1
, we have
\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J-1}(C+1)}\geq\tfrac{\sigma}{\sqrt{N}}
and
\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J-1}(C+1)}\geq C_{1}\sigma\epsilon\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}
. Lemma
D.4
implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta_{J^{\prime}-1}^{2}+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}\exp\left(-\tfrac{NC_{9}\eta_{J^{\prime}-1}^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)+\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N},

and then we repeat the proof of Case 2(b)(ii) in the bounded sub-Gaussian version to argue the bound in
\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}
above reduces to
\lesssim\tfrac{\sigma^{2}}{N}
. But we also know
\eta_{J^{\ast}}\geq\tfrac{\sigma\log 2}{\sqrt{N}}
by definition of
J^{\ast}
, so
\mathbb{E}_{R}\mathbb{E}_{X}\|\mu-\nu^{\ast\ast}\|^{2}\lesssim\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2}\lesssim\max(\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2},\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2}\log(1/\epsilon))
.

Case 3:
Suppose
\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J^{\prime}-1}(C+1)}<\tfrac{\sigma}{\sqrt{N}}
never occurs for
J^{\prime}\leq J^{\ast}
, but at some point
1\leq J^{\prime}\leq J^{\ast}
we have
\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J^{\prime}-1}(C+1)}<C_{1}\sigma\epsilon\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}
. Then repeat the proof of Case 2(b)(i) using index
J^{\prime}
instead of
J^{\prime\prime}
, i.e., let
J^{\prime}
be the minimal index such that
\tfrac{d_{m}}{2^{J^{\prime}-1}(C+1)}<C_{1}\sigma\epsilon\sqrt{\log(1/\epsilon)}
(since we may apply Lemma
D.4
with
J^{\prime}
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.12
.

The
\eta^{\ast}=0
edge-case cannot happen as argued in the proof of Theorem
5.10
.

Next, without modifications, the argument from Case 1 in the proof of Theorem
5.10
that
{\eta^{\ast}}^{2}
is a lower bound carries through (this portion did not use an
\epsilon\geq\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{N}}
assumption and still works for sub-Gaussian noise). By the remarks in Section
5.1
about extending Lemma
2.3
, we also have
\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2}\log(1/\epsilon)
as a lower bound. Hence,
\max({\eta^{\ast}}^{2},\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2}\log(1/\epsilon))
is a lower bound.

For the upper bound, recall from Remark
3.9
that we can swap out the
2c
in (
5.5
) with
c
in the following proof. Moreover, as in the proof of Theorem
5.10
, we can also drop the
\log 2
term for sufficiently large
c
. Theorem (
5.11
) implies
\max(\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2},\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2}\log(1/\epsilon))
is an upper bound. Now repeat the argument in Case 1 of the proof of Theorem
4.8
with
\beta^{\prime}=\min(\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}},\tfrac{c}{2}\sqrt{\tfrac{2}{C_{5}}})\in(0,1/\sqrt{3}]
(for the same reason as in Theorem
5.10
), with the same
\tilde{\eta}
and
\phi
defined without a
\log 2
term and a cubic power instead. The argument implies
\tilde{\eta}^{2}\geq\eta_{J^{\ast}}^{2}
where
\tilde{\eta}\asymp\eta^{\ast}
, and hence
\max(\eta_{\ast}^{2},\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2}\log(1/\epsilon))
is an upper bound.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5.14
.

We apply the sparse Varshamov-Gilbert Lemma given in
Rigollet and Hütter [2023, Lemma 4.14]
. This yields
\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{M}\in\{0,1\}^{n}
where each
\omega_{i}
has
s
non-zero coordinates,
\omega_{i}
and
\omega_{j}
disagree on at least
s/2
coordinates for
i\neq j
, and
\log M\geq\tfrac{s}{8}\log(1+\tfrac{n}{2s})
. Define
\omega_{i}^{\prime}=\omega_{i}\delta/\sqrt{s}
, and then observe by
s
-sparsity and
\omega_{i}
being binary that
\|\omega_{i}^{\prime}\|^{2}=\delta^{2}
. Thus,
\omega_{i}^{\prime}\in B(0,\delta)\cap K
, using a closed ball. Moreover, for
i\neq j
we have

\displaystyle\|\omega_{i}^{\prime}-\omega_{j}^{\prime}\|^{2}=\tfrac{\delta^{2}}{s}\|\omega_{i}-\omega_{j}\|^{2}\geq\tfrac{\delta^{2}}{s}\cdot\tfrac{s}{2}=\tfrac{\delta^{2}}{2}>\tfrac{\delta^{2}}{c^{2}},

for
c>\sqrt{2}
. Thus, we have a
\delta/c
-packing of
B(0,\delta)\cap K
of log cardinality
\geq\tfrac{s}{8}\log(1+\tfrac{n}{2s})
, proving the lower bound.

Now define the local Gaussian width at a point
\nu
by

w_{\nu}(\delta)=\mathbb{E}_{Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\mathbb{I})}\bigg[\sup_{y\in B(\nu,\delta)\cap K}Z{{}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}y\bigg].

Then by a Sudakov minoration argument
[
Wainwright, 2019
, Theorem 5.30]
, we have
\log\mathcal{M}_{K}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\delta,c)\lesssim\sup_{\nu\in K}\tfrac{[w_{\nu}(\delta)]^{2}}{\delta^{2}}.
Given any subset
S\subseteq[n]
with
|S|=2s
, write
Z_{S}
to be equal to the random vector
Z
but with coordinates
i\in S
set to
0
and coordinates
i\not\in S
left unchanged. Note that if
y,\nu
are both
s
-sparse, then
y-\nu
is at most
2s
-sparse. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z}\bigg[\sup_{y\in B(\nu,\delta)\cap K}Z{{}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}y\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z}\bigg[\sup_{y\in B(\nu,\delta)\cap K}Z{{}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}(y-\nu)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z}\bigg[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[n]\\
|S|=2s\end{subarray}}\sup_{y\in B(\nu,\delta)\cap K}Z_{S}{{}^{\mathrm{\scriptscriptstyle T}}}(y-\nu)\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{Z}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[n]\\
|S|=2s\end{subarray}}\sup_{y\in B(\nu,\delta)\cap K}\|Z_{S}\|\|y-\nu\|

\displaystyle\leq\delta\cdot\mathbb{E}_{Z}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[n]\\
|S|=2s\end{subarray}}\|Z_{S}\|

\displaystyle\leq\delta\cdot\mathbb{E}_{Z}\sqrt{\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{2s}Z_{(i)}^{2}},

where we write the coordinates of
Z
in order of non-increasing absolute value, say
|Z_{(1)}|\geq|Z_{(2)}|\geq\dots\geq|Z_{(n)}|
.

Now, recall from Lemma
5.1
that
\mathbb{P}(\|W\|>R)\leq 25^{s}\exp(-R^{2}/8)
if
W
is an
2s
-dimensional sub-Gaussian with parameter
1
. Now if
\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{2s}Z_{(i)}^{2}}>R
, that means one of the
\binom{n}{2s}
many choices of coordinates of
Z
was chosen, and the norm of those
2s
coordinates exceeds
R
. Hence, a union bound and binomial coefficient bound implies

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{2s}Z_{(i)}^{2}}\geq R\right)

\displaystyle\leq\binom{n}{2s}25^{s}\exp(-\tfrac{R^{2}}{8})

\displaystyle\leq\left[\left(\tfrac{en}{2s}\right)^{2s}25^{s}\exp(-\tfrac{R^{2}}{8})\right]\wedge 1,

noting the bound is only relevant when
\leq 1
. Moreover, note that for
R>R^{\prime}
where
R^{\prime}=\sqrt{8s\log 25+16s\log(en/(2s))}
, we have
\left(\tfrac{en}{2s}\right)^{2s}25^{s}\exp(-{R}^{2}/8)\leq 1
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sqrt{\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{2s}Z_{(i)}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{s}Z_{(i)}^{2}}\geq R\right)\mathrm{d}R

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{R^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{2s}Z_{(i)}^{2}}\geq R\right)\mathrm{d}R+\int_{R^{\prime}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(\sqrt{\sum\nolimits_{i=1}^{2s}Z_{(i)}^{2}}\geq R\right)\mathrm{d}R

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{R^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}R+\int_{R^{\prime}}^{\infty}\left(\tfrac{en}{2s}\right)^{2s}25^{s}\exp(-R^{2}/8)\mathrm{d}R

\displaystyle=R^{\prime}+\left(\tfrac{en}{2s}\right)^{2s}25^{s}\int_{R^{\prime}}^{\infty}\exp(-R^{2}/8)\mathrm{d}R

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{\prime}+\left(\tfrac{en}{2s}\right)^{2s}25^{s}\exp(-{R^{\prime}}^{2}/8)\lesssim R^{\prime}+1\lesssim R^{\prime}.

Thus

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{M}_{K}^{\operatorname{loc}}(\delta,c)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{\nu\in K}\frac{[w_{\nu}(\delta)]^{2}}{\delta^{2}}\leq\frac{\left[\delta\cdot\mathbb{E}_{Z}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{2s}Z_{(i)}^{2}}]\right]^{2}}{\delta^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{R^{\prime}}^{2}\asymp s\log(1+\tfrac{n}{2s}).

∎
